## Group 3 (starts row 14)

### Row 14

- **A14** (Group plugin): machinery
- **B14** (New rule): A SPEC: prompt is an authoritative specification to implement, framed by the work in flight and tied to existing tickets. When one is captured or pending, run /machinery:spec-intake from a root session (in an isolated copy say so and stop): file it into the specification under docs/dictated-specs/ that owns the subsystem, keeping its substance (the directory is fixed and created if absent; SPEC_INDEX.md lives in .claude/machinery/), commit with the regenerated index and disposition via intake.mjs, or dismiss a non-specification with its reason, and report file § Section. Work is dispatched only after filing, citing the specification by path rather than restating it.
- **C14** (Existing rule): M493: ${specs} specification${specs === 1 ? '' : 's'} pending in the spec inbox — running the spec intake (skill: machinery:spec-intake) before this prompt.

### Row 15 (new rule in B14)

- **A15** (Group plugin): machinery
- **B15** (New rule): _empty_
- **C15** (Existing rule): M283: - A prompt beginning `SPEC:` carries a specification to implement, not a rule about behaviour and not a proposal to weigh. Treat it as the authoritative design a principal architect hands down: it is framed by the work, feature or campaign currently in flight or just discussed, and it has associated tickets to find rather than to invent. It lands in the specification that owns that subsystem, never in a rules file, before any work is dispatched on it — and a dispatch then cites that specification by path rather than restating it.

- **B49** (New rule): When defining a standing agent, declare it in one file: the one question it answers, its triggers, its exact tools and its model. ## Group 9 (starts row 54)

### Row 54

- **A54** (Group plugin): machinery
- **B54** (New rule): When defining or dispatching an agent, contain it by removing capabilities, not by prompt rules. A judging agent gets no command or write access and its definition states the consequence: the caller supplies the scope (diff text or changed-file list), and without one it reports a blocked run and stops, never auditing the whole codebase, guessing scope or offering a partial sweep. An agent that must build and run states the weakness and gets an allowlist of build, test, harness and read-only inspection commands. Verifying and reviewing agents are forbidden by name every command that moves a reference or mutates the copy (branch create/move/delete, merge, force-reset, push, checkout, reset, rebase, commit, stash, adding or removing a copy) and never stage the owner's protected records. A parallel implementation agent owns one directory and writes only inside it.
- **C54** (Existing rule): M29: - An agent that only judges is given no ability to run commands or write files, so it cannot move or damage anything. The capability is removed, not forbidden: a rule in the prompt is a promise.
- **D54** (SDLC stage): DESIGN
- **E54** (Kind): AGENTS
- **F54** (Test type): _empty_
- **G54** (Plugin): machinery
- **H54** (Location): plugins/machinery/rules/agent-topology.md § Containment is structural
- **I54** (Trigger): defining a judge-only agent

### Row 55 (new rule in B54)

- **A55** (Group plugin): machinery
- **B55** (New rule): _empty_
- **C55** (Existing rule): M30: - Removing a capability has a consequence, and the agent's own definition states it. An agent that cannot work out its own scope has it supplied by the caller; given none, it says so and stops. It never substitutes the whole codebase, and never guesses the scope from timestamps or file contents.
- **D55** (SDLC stage): DESIGN
- **E55** (Kind): AGENTS
- **F55** (Test type): _empty_
- **G55** (Plugin): machinery
- **H55** (Location): plugins/machinery/rules/agent-topology.md § Containment is structural
- **I55** (Trigger): removing an agent capability

### Row 56 (new rule in B54)

- **A56** (Group plugin): machinery

## Group 10 (starts row 66)

### Row 66

- **A66** (Group plugin): unbreakable
- **B66** (New rule): For every design decision and code path, in any language, apply cant-break-by-design: name the invariant in one sentence before writing the path (if you cannot, the design is not ready) and enforce it with a mechanism at the highest rung the language allows. Rungs 7-8 are the target (a sole-constructor type is the standard, deleting the possibility is the goal); a shared helper is the weakest acceptable form and carries promotion debt; rungs 0-4 support a mechanism and are never the enforcement. Test: a call site added by someone who read nothing is still correct. Duplicating a processing step at a second call site means the design is wrong: stop and build the choke point. Where the language is weak, move the invariant to codegen, build-time checks, or one sealed module with a tiny surface backed by lints.
- **C66** (Existing rule): M49: - The `cant-break-by-design` skill is mandatory: it is invoked for every design decision and every code path, in any language, not consulted when someone remembers it. Its rule is the one every other hard rule here is an instance of — every design decision makes the bad state structurally impossible rather than forbidden by a rule someone has to remember, by a mechanism and never by a prose "must".
- **D66** (SDLC stage): DESIGN
- **E66** (Kind): DESIGN
- **F66** (Test type): _empty_
- **G66** (Plugin): machinery
- **H66** (Location): plugins/machinery/rules/design-invariants.md § The mandate
- **I66** (Trigger): every design decision and code path

### Row 67 (new rule in B66)

- **A67** (Group plugin): unbreakable
- **B67** (New rule): _empty_
- **C67** (Existing rule): U1: Use when designing or writing ANY code path, in any language — enforces the absolute rule that invariants must be unrepresentable-by-construction (one pipeline per invariant, bypass = compile error); includes the enforcement ladder, 15 techniques, per-language strongest tools, the tripwire (duplicating a step at a 2nd call site = design already wrong), and 19 anti-patterns that catch invariants whose stated strength exceeds their real mechanism (pub fields under a sole-constructor claim, debug_assert as enforcement, caller preconditions in prose, run-scoped globals, tests standing in for construction)
- **D67** (SDLC stage): DESIGN
- **E67** (Kind): SKILL-TRIGGER
- **F67** (Test type): _empty_
- **G67** (Plugin): unbreakable
- **H67** (Location): pure-prose/unbreakable/cant-break-by-design/SKILL.md § frontmatter
- **I67** (Trigger): designing or writing any code path

## Group 12 (starts row 88)

Debt is allowed; silent debt is a finding. ## Group 13 (starts row 100)

### Row 100

- **A100** (Group plugin): machinery
- **B100** (New rule): When a type's invariant rests on how it is constructed: create it at the authority that reads the source of truth (never wrapped by hand at call sites), in its own unit, or in a dependency-free shared vocabulary unit when creator and consumer are independent. Fields are private with one blessed factory; block generated empty-value and decoder constructors with a private marker member; keep source checks that the type and marker stay put. Write the compile-fail tests (field read, direct build, cross-assignment) red first, then make the fields private in the same change. Two meanings get two types (or names with different return types), and range limits live in one constructor or range type, never in every setter. [Superseded by owner ruling: TDD order — compile-fail proofs are written red before the fields go private]
- **C100** (Existing rule): M51: - A distinguishing type is only as strong as the place it is created. Wrapped by hand at each call site, two adjacent wrappings can be swapped and the type catches nothing. Create it at the authority that reads the source of truth, so the source, the direction and the type are declared in one place.
- **D100** (SDLC stage): DESIGN
- **E100** (Kind): DESIGN
- **F100** (Test type): _empty_
- **G100** (Plugin): machinery
- **H100** (Location): plugins/machinery/rules/design-invariants.md § Where a distinguishing type is created
- **I100** (Trigger): creating a distinguishing type

### Row 101 (new rule in B100)

- **A101** (Group plugin): machinery
- **B101** (New rule): _empty_
- **C101** (Existing rule): M52: - When the place that creates such a type and the place that consumes it do not depend on each other, the type goes in a shared vocabulary unit with no dependencies of its own. Making one depend on the other pays for the invariant with whatever property that unit was built to have.
- **D101** (SDLC stage): DESIGN
- **E101** (Kind): DESIGN
- **F101** (Test type): _empty_
- **G101** (Plugin): machinery
- **H101** (Location): plugins/machinery/rules/design-invariants.md § Where a distinguishing type is created
- **I101** (Trigger): type creator and consumer independent

## Group 15 (starts row 110)

### Row 110

- **A110** (Group plugin): tie: machinery / unbreakable
- **B110** (New rule): Compute each fact, bound or classification once, where it is owned or produced, and have everything else read it; never store a value twice, keep copies "beside each other", or declare a bound in both a schema and a constructor. A stored or transmitted format states the fact outright so no consumer has to infer it.
- **C110** (Existing rule): M62: - A fact is computed once, at the place that owns it, and everything else reads it. Nothing downstream recomputes its own opinion of a fact that already exists, because a second derivation will eventually disagree with the first, and that disagreement arrives looking exactly like a defect.
- **D110** (SDLC stage): DESIGN
- **E110** (Kind): DESIGN
- **F110** (Test type): _empty_
- **G110** (Plugin): machinery
- **H110** (Location): plugins/machinery/rules/design-invariants.md § Never re-derive a fact
- **I110** (Trigger): computing a fact

- **A111** (Group plugin): tie: machinery / unbreakable
- **B111** (New rule): _empty_
- **C111** (Existing rule): M63: - In a stored or transmitted format, the producer states the fact outright. If a consumer has to infer it by arithmetic, the format is wrong: it has forced the same re-derivation on every reader, forever.
- **D111** (SDLC stage): DESIGN
- **E111** (Kind): DESIGN
- **F111** (Test type): _empty_
- **G111** (Plugin): machinery

## Group 16 (starts row 116)

### Row 116

- **A116** (Group plugin): machinery
- **B116** (New rule): When several artifacts must agree (a mirror table, a hand-kept list, a switch name spelled in several places): make one the declaration and generate the rest. Each environment switch is resolved once, by the unit that owns the fact it arms; everything else reads the resolved field, and per-item decisions choose their path once outside the loop. Only where no generator is possible, back the single declaration with a source scan (per unit plus one project-wide sweep that also covers units that never opted in). [Resolves conflict: U63, M71, M74, M75 — generator first; drift scan only where no generator exists; owner to confirm]
- **C116** (Existing rule): M70: - The process environment is a fact like any other: every switch is resolved once, at one authority per unit, and everything inside reads a field. The switch's name appearing anywhere else in that unit is a defect. How often it is read is not the criterion; where it is read is.
- **D116** (SDLC stage): DESIGN
- **E116** (Kind): DESIGN
- **F116** (Test type): _empty_
- **G116** (Plugin): machinery
- **H116** (Location): plugins/machinery/rules/design-invariants.md § One authority per switch
- **I116** (Trigger): a process-environment switch

### Row 117 (new rule in B116)

- **A117** (Group plugin): machinery
- **B117** (New rule): _empty_
- **C117** (Existing rule): M73: - One authority per unit is where a switch starts, not permission to claim a name another unit already owns. A switch two units honour is resolved in the one that owns the fact it arms, and the others read the resolved value as data. Reading it is not re-deriving; parsing the name a second time is.
- **D117** (SDLC stage): DESIGN
- **E117** (Kind): DESIGN
- **F117** (Test type): _empty_

- **H117** (Location): plugins/machinery/rules/design-invariants.md § One authority per switch
- **I117** (Trigger): switch honoured by two units

## Group 18 (starts row 127)

### Row 127

- **A127** (Group plugin): unbreakable
- **B127** (New rule): When a function depends on a precondition, make it a type: parse at the boundary into a type carrying the proof, accept only types buildable from conforming input, and make the guarded thing the only reachable currency. If that is not worth doing, record the invariant against the caller and say "unchecked" in the prose. "Cannot fail in practice" is proved from the types or returned as an error, never an unchecked unwrap.
- **C127** (Existing rule): U18: 2. **Parse, don't validate** — validation returns a *new* type carrying the proof; after the boundary the unchecked value does not exist.
- **D127** (SDLC stage): DESIGN

- **F127** (Test type): _empty_

## Group 28 (starts row 143)

### Row 143

- **A143** (Group plugin): machinery
- **B143** (New rule): When declaring a setting, its class fixes its default and accessor: a first-class (directly set) value declares a default and returns a plain value whose absence is impossible by construction; a second-class (computed) value has no default and never falls back to a raw lookup; a third-class optional override (also free text, actions, templates) has no default and returns an optional. If absence means "inherit another setting", declare no default; if it means "zero elements of this collection", declare the empty value (matching the reference format), which is not a stand-in value. Never default everything.
- **C143** (Existing rule): M78: - The ban on stand-in values covers a legal value made to stand for a different concept; it never covers a collection's own emptiness.
- **D143** (SDLC stage): DESIGN
- **E143** (Kind): DESIGN
- **F143** (Test type): _empty_
- **G143** (Plugin): machinery
- **H143** (Location): plugins/machinery/rules/design-invariants.md § Absence and defaults
- **I143** (Trigger): judging a stand-in value

### Row 144 (new rule in B143)

- **A144** (Group plugin): machinery
- **B144** (New rule): _empty_
- **C144** (Existing rule): M79: - Which of the two shapes absence takes is fixed by the setting's class, not by the author's taste: an optional is reserved for the case where absence is the signal.
- **D144** (SDLC stage): DESIGN
- **E144** (Kind): DESIGN
- **F144** (Test type): _empty_
- **G144** (Plugin): machinery

### Row 145 (new rule in B143)

- **A145** (Group plugin): machinery
- **B145** (New rule): _empty_
- **C145** (Existing rule): M80: - An empty collection used as a default is not a stand-in value: a magic number meaning "inherit" smuggles a different concept inside a legal value, whereas an empty list decoding to zero elements simply is the value. One question separates them: does absence mean inherit another setting's value, or zero elements of this setting's own collection?
- **D145** (SDLC stage): DESIGN
- **E145** (Kind): DESIGN
- **F145** (Test type): _empty_
- **G145** (Plugin): machinery
- **H145** (Location): plugins/machinery/rules/design-invariants.md § Absence and defaults

- **A146** (Group plugin): machinery
- **B146** (New rule): _empty_
- **C146** (Existing rule): M81: - If absence means inherit another setting's value, declare no default at all, ever. A fabricated default there silently severs the inheritance, which is the exact damage this rule exists to prevent.
- **D146** (SDLC stage): DESIGN

- **B147** (New rule): _empty_
- **C147** (Existing rule): M82: - If absence means zero elements of that setting's own collection, declare the empty value as its default, matching the reference format's own empty defaults. Where the definitions are themselves declared in a table the build checks, withholding it makes that table fail to build, which is the mechanism doing the enforcing rather than a reviewer.

## Group 29 (starts row 152)

### Row 152

- **A152** (Group plugin): machinery
- **B152** (New rule): When a pipeline stage consumes a resource, remove exactly what it produced or claimed from what later stages can receive, before anything downstream is derived. A stage returning an unexercised claim gives it back to its original owner, once per site; leftovers from an exercised claim go forward to the one successor the design names, recorded, ending in use or a declared, warned discard. The two declines never share a word, and every declined resource has exactly one named recipient. An audit first asks which two stages received the same resource.
- **C152** (Existing rule): M88: - Once a stage consumes a resource, that resource is permanently removed from what any later stage can receive. The same resource reaching two consuming stages is a defect class that keeps recurring, and this is the rule that finds it.
- **D152** (SDLC stage): DESIGN
- **E152** (Kind): DESIGN
- **F152** (Test type): _empty_
- **G152** (Plugin): machinery
- **H152** (Location): plugins/machinery/rules/design-invariants.md § Handing a resource on

- **A153** (Group plugin): machinery
- **B153** (New rule): _empty_

## Group 30 (starts row 160)

### Row 160

- **A160** (Group plugin): machinery
- **B160** (New rule): When reading a reference format, never narrow its collection type to a single value; if you cannot read the collection form yet, write an accessor that could or record the gap. Accessors keep the source's name, with plurality in the type. Judge one-versus-many against the data model actually in front of the reader (a store that already routes one value per owner is read as a single value).
- **C160** (Existing rule): M103: - Never narrow a reference format's collection type to a single value because it obviously holds only one. That is a judgement call about someone else's data model, and those lose. The schema describes their format, not your reader, and narrowing it silently drops data on a round trip.
- **D160** (SDLC stage): DESIGN
- **E160** (Kind): DESIGN
- **F160** (Test type): _empty_
- **G160** (Plugin): machinery
- **H160** (Location): plugins/machinery/rules/design-invariants.md § Reading someone else's data model
- **I160** (Trigger): reading a reference format's collection

### Row 161 (new rule in B160)

- **A161** (Group plugin): machinery
- **B161** (New rule): _empty_
- **C161** (Existing rule): M104: - If you cannot read the collection form yet, write an accessor that could, or record the gap. Never write a schema that lies in order to flatter your reader.

- **E161** (Kind): DESIGN

- **F162** (Test type): _empty_

Where the store has already reduced a key to one value per owner and routes it to the owner it belongs to, the plurality lives in that routing, and reading it as a single value is the plural-correct read; reading it as a collection returns nothing on every load and lets a fallback win instead.
- **D163** (SDLC stage): IMPL

## Group 32 (starts row 165)

### Row 165

- **A165** (Group plugin): machinery
- **B165** (New rule): Declare each diagnostic code once, in one entry carrying its documentation, a literal external name and its severity level; generate the code list, external format and level table from it. Call sites never pass their own level, and levels must mean something.
- **C165** (Existing rule): M116: - How serious a diagnostic is gets decided once, for each diagnostic code, in one place. The sites that raise it never pass a level of their own, so the same condition cannot come out as a warning in one caller and a passing remark in another.
- **D165** (SDLC stage): DESIGN
- **E165** (Kind): INSTRUMENTATION
- **F165** (Test type): _empty_
- **G165** (Plugin): machinery
- **H165** (Location): plugins/machinery/rules/design-invariants.md § What a diagnostic and a measurement may claim

### Row 166 (new rule in B165)

- **A166** (Group plugin): machinery
- **B166** (New rule): _empty_
- **C166** (Existing rule): M117: - Each diagnostic code is declared once, in a single entry carrying its documentation, its external name and its level together, and everything else — the list of codes, the external format, the level table — is generated from that entry. A list kept by hand beside it is the denominator of the test that guards the format, and a missing line there compiles cleanly while quietly narrowing the guard. The external name stays a written-out literal rather than being derived from the code's own name, so renaming a code fails loudly instead of silently rewriting every message already in the field.
- **D166** (SDLC stage): DESIGN
- **E166** (Kind): INSTRUMENTATION
- **F166** (Test type): META

## Group 34 (starts row 169)

### Row 169

- **A169** (Group plugin): machinery
- **B169** (New rule): When building an instrument or measurement: ship it in the same build as the change it verifies. A timer wraps only the work, never the test deciding whether the work runs. A cost printed to the user comes from its own clock read once at that site, never a default-off profiler; a per-run measurement carries its counts and their total together in one value.
- **C169** (Existing rule): M121: - A measurement of one run is a value that run produces, never a running total that outlives it. The counts and the total they are read against travel together inside that one value, so a second run in the same process cannot report the first run's work over its own count.

- **E169** (Kind): INSTRUMENTATION

## Group 35 (starts row 174)

### Row 174

- **A174** (Group plugin): unbreakable
- **B174** (New rule): When storing data, derive storage from the source and presentation from each consumer: keep every distinction the source made at the platform's native maximum fidelity, store values not renderings, keep typed text as entered (normalise only a derived key), keep originals with derivatives beside them, aggregate raw series on read, and never claim more precision than the source gave. Requirements flow source to store to view, never back; a presentation need (e.g. 1-based ids) converts once at the boundary while internals keep the language's native conventions. Reduce fidelity only when privacy, law or real cost requires it: late, deliberately, and on the record with the reason.
- **C174** (Existing rule): M122: - Never replace the base programming language's own convention — zero-based indexing, its native ordering, its own numbering — to match a presentation-layer feature. A presentation requirement, such as a 1-based id shown to a user or a reference format's own 1-based numbering, converts once at the boundary that produces it — the persisted format, the display — and internals keep the language's native convention throughout.
- **D174** (SDLC stage): DESIGN
- **E174** (Kind): DESIGN
- **F174** (Test type): _empty_
- **G174** (Plugin): machinery
- **H174** (Location): plugins/machinery/rules/design-invariants.md § The base language's own convention
- **I174** (Trigger): presentation needs other numbering

### Row 175 (new rule in B174)

- **A175** (Group plugin): unbreakable
- **B175** (New rule): _empty_
- **C175** (Existing rule): U99: - *Storage derives from the source.* Keep every distinction the source made, at the maximum fidelity the ecosystem holds **natively, by default** — the strongest representation the platform and store already speak, not an exotic one you construct. Native and maximal, in that order: a custom type for digits the platform does not speak buys nothing and pays at every boundary.
- **D175** (SDLC stage): DESIGN
- **E175** (Kind): DESIGN
- **F175** (Test type): _empty_
- **G175** (Plugin): unbreakable
- **H175** (Location): pure-prose/unbreakable/be-reasonable/SKILL.md § 2. The worked example: storage and presentation
- **I175** (Trigger): storing source data

### Row 176 (new rule in B174)

- **A176** (Group plugin): unbreakable
- **B176** (New rule): _empty_
- **C176** (Existing rule): U100: - *Presentation derives from the consumer,* and there is rarely one consumer. A person scanning a report, a person reconstructing the order of events, a machine reading an interface, and an export someone will compute on are four readers of one field, and they want different projections. That is not a conflict to resolve in the schema; it is four formatters over one store.
- **D176** (SDLC stage): DESIGN
- **E176** (Kind): DESIGN
- **F176** (Test type): _empty_
- **G176** (Plugin): unbreakable
- **H176** (Location): pure-prose/unbreakable/be-reasonable/SKILL.md § 2. The worked example: storage and presentation
- **I176** (Trigger): presenting stored data

### Row 177 (new rule in B174)

- **A177** (Group plugin): unbreakable
- **B177** (New rule): _empty_
- **C177** (Existing rule): U101: - **The arrow runs one way.** Requirements flow source → store → view, never view → store. "The report only shows minutes, so store minutes" is the entire failure in one clause: one reader, present at the moment of writing, setting the ceiling for every reader who comes later.
- **D177** (SDLC stage): DESIGN
- **E177** (Kind): DESIGN
- **F177** (Test type): _empty_
- **G177** (Plugin): unbreakable
- **H177** (Location): pure-prose/unbreakable/be-reasonable/SKILL.md § 2. The worked example: storage and presentation
- **I177** (Trigger): a view's needs shape storage

### Row 178 (new rule in B174)

- **A178** (Group plugin): unbreakable
- **B178** (New rule): _empty_
- **C178** (Existing rule): U102: - **Claim no more than the source gave.** Padding a coarse clock with zeros does not add precision, it adds *false* precision — worse than coarseness, because downstream it is indistinguishable from the real thing.

- **E178** (Kind): DESIGN

## Group 37 (starts row 188)

- **A188** (Group plugin): machinery
- **B188** (New rule): Never add an external dependency without the owner's explicit authorisation. Weigh it by transitive surface, update cadence and removal cost (not download size), preferring few transitive dependencies. Installs use the frozen lock file only, block lifecycle scripts except an allowlist, require a stated minimum published age, and use an installer pinned to a stated major version.
- **C188** (Existing rule): M130: - Package lifecycle scripts are blocked by default, with an explicit allowlist naming each package permitted to run one.
- **D188** (SDLC stage): DESIGN
- **E188** (Kind): TOOLING

## Group 39 (starts row 201)

### Row 201

- **A201** (Group plugin): unbreakable
- **B201** (New rule): While writing code, when two answers remain defensible after deriving and the difference is visible to whoever uses the result, ask the developer directing the work (not the end user, and never by inventing a setting; raise a runtime setting only if one is genuinely warranted). Offer only options that exist, starting at the true maximum, in two steps: the full or default form? if not, which real alternative? Ask once and apply the answer across the whole surface; never ask about what you could derive or discover in seconds.
- **C201** (Existing rule): U96: A choice is genuinely open when **two answers are defensible after deriving**, and **the difference is visible** to whoever uses the result.
- **D201** (SDLC stage): DESIGN
- **E201** (Kind): COMMUNICATION
- **F201** (Test type): _empty_
- **G201** (Plugin): unbreakable
- **H201** (Location): pure-prose/unbreakable/be-reasonable/SKILL.md § 1.4 Ask when it is genuinely open
- **I201** (Trigger): two defensible visible answers remain

### Row 202 (new rule in B201)

- **A202** (Group plugin): unbreakable
- **B202** (New rule): _empty_
- **C202** (Existing rule): U85: 4. **Ask when it is genuinely open.** Two defensible answers, and the difference is visible to whoever uses the thing: **ask the developer you are working with, while you are writing the code** — not the end user, not by inventing a setting — and offer only the options that actually exist.
- **D202** (SDLC stage): IMPL
- **E202** (Kind): COMMUNICATION
- **F202** (Test type): _empty_
- **G202** (Plugin): unbreakable
- **H202** (Location): pure-prose/unbreakable/be-reasonable/SKILL.md § Skill: derive the choice, lean toward the cheaper mistake, split two masters, ask when it's genuinely open
- **I202** (Trigger): two defensible answers with visible difference

- **A203** (Group plugin): unbreakable

- **B207** (New rule): _empty_
- **C207** (Existing rule): U120: **How: bounded by what exists.** The menu you offer is the set of real options, starting at the true maximum available. Offering a precision the source cannot produce, a guarantee the system cannot make, or a mode that does not exist is a false statement about the system, and every answer built on it is wrong before it is given.

## Group 40 (starts row 212)

### Row 212

- **A212** (Group plugin): machinery
- **B212** (New rule): A long-running tool prints a heartbeat on its own output (upstream of any log redirect) at a bounded, regular interval, in one project-wide shape: a fixed liveness token, the tool name, elapsed time, and progress with a denominator where knowable; a fixture proves it survives the output filter in every mode. Missing an interval is a defect in the tool, and when waiting on a job, silence past the interval means dead or hung.
- **C212** (Existing rule): M188: - The shape: a single line beginning with a fixed liveness token, then the tool's name, elapsed time, and optional progress (a denominator preferred over a bare pulse), emitted at a bounded, regular interval. The literal token and the interval are a project's own choice, fixed once and matched by one pattern everywhere.
- **D212** (SDLC stage): DESIGN
- **E212** (Kind): TOOLING
- **F212** (Test type): _empty_
- **G212** (Plugin): machinery
- **H212** (Location): plugins/machinery/rules/tool-output.md § Heartbeats
- **I212** (Trigger): defining heartbeat shape

- **B213** (New rule): _empty_

It names the tool, shows it is alive, and gives progress where progress is knowable, because a count beats a bare pulse.
- **D213** (SDLC stage): IMPL

## Group 42 (starts row 220)

### Row 220

- **A220** (Group plugin): machinery
- **B220** (New rule): Every new piece of work gets a ticket a competent stranger could pick up cold, with its plans and specs hanging off it. When asked to file or repair a pair, do it as one instruction: create the ticket (problem, numbered required behaviour, design constraint, dated decision and who made it, tests required, exact code places), create its companion titled for it, link them with the dedicated companion link type (distinct from ordering or dependency links; nothing else is a child), and label both with the working copy. Link a pair belonging to an effort at both levels (ticket to ticket, companion to companion). A ticket number means the pair.
- **C220** (Existing rule): M254: - The link that binds a ticket to its companion must be a different kind of relationship from the links that express ordering or dependency between an effort's tickets and the work tickets under it — different enough that a query tells the two apart by the relationship's own type, never by remembering which values of a shared field mean which. The tracker then cannot confuse a companion with a dependency.
- **D220** (SDLC stage): DESIGN
- **E220** (Kind): TICKETS
- **F220** (Test type): _empty_
- **G220** (Plugin): machinery
- **H220** (Location): plugins/machinery/rules/work-tracking.md § A ticket and its companion
- **I220** (Trigger): configuring the tracker link types

## Group 43 (starts row 229)

### Row 229

- **A229** (Group plugin): machinery
- **B229** (New rule): When starting a feature or fix, before any code, write in its ticket: the beliefs its design rests on; the invariants it touches and introduces; what it will affect and what it must leave alone; the standard it claims (identical output or a deliberate behaviour change, never a behaviour change called cleanup); and the list of checks it requires. Each listed check runs on its own; one passing never excuses another.
- **C229** (Existing rule): U51: 8. **Both directions.** Enumerate the invariants the feature TOUCHES and the ones it INTRODUCES. Entering a sealed pipeline correctly does not make the output deserve to exist.

- **B244** (New rule): When creating a working copy, use the worktree tool, not a hand command: name the copy for the work (not a date or ticket number) and the branch identically with no prefix, which the tool enforces by stripping harness prefixes and refusing an empty name. ## Group 46 (starts row 252)

### Row 252

- **A252** (Group plugin): unbreakable
- **B252** (New rule): When making a design choice that is not an invariant (precision, defaults, timeouts, retries, naming, config, logging, error text, test level, caching, deploy shape, output format), state it as a question with at least two answers and apply the four moves: derive it, lean toward the cheaper mistake, split two masters, ask when genuinely open. Test: can you say in one sentence what you paid, what you bought, and what here made it favourable, without "best practice"? Invariants are not traded this way; they go to the highest rung.
- **C252** (Existing rule): U81: Use when making any design choice that is not an invariant — precision, defaults, timeouts, retries, naming, config, logging, error text, test level, cache policy, deploy shape, output format, and who runs the tooling under a context budget; enforces four moves (derive the choice from the situation, lean toward the mistake that is cheaper to undo, split any decision serving two masters, ask the developer when two answers are genuinely defensible and offer only options that exist); includes the storage-vs-presentation worked example, how to read an asymmetry, and a domain appendix showing the method already applied
- **D252** (SDLC stage): DESIGN
- **E252** (Kind): SKILL-TRIGGER
- **F252** (Test type): _empty_
- **G252** (Plugin): unbreakable
- **H252** (Location): pure-prose/unbreakable/be-reasonable/SKILL.md § frontmatter

## Group 48 (starts row 264)

### Row 264

- **A264** (Group plugin): unbreakable
- **B264** (New rule): When uncertainty remains, read the asymmetry and take the option whose error is cheaper: first which error can be undone, by whom and at what cost; then which is discovered (a crash beats a plausible wrong result); then which compounds (compounding beats a larger one-time cost); then who pays, correcting deliberately for costs exported to others.
- **C264** (Existing rule): U83: 2. **Lean toward the cheaper mistake.** Where you are uncertain, take the option whose error is recoverable, loud, and paid by you rather than silent, permanent, and paid by someone downstream.
- **D264** (SDLC stage): DESIGN
- **E264** (Kind): DESIGN
- **F264** (Test type): _empty_
- **G264** (Plugin): unbreakable
- **H264** (Location): pure-prose/unbreakable/be-reasonable/SKILL.md § Skill: derive the choice, lean toward the cheaper mistake, split two masters, ask when it's genuinely open
- **I264** (Trigger): uncertain design choice

- **A265** (Group plugin): unbreakable
- **B265** (New rule): _empty_
- **C265** (Existing rule): U94: Under uncertainty, take the option whose error costs less to discover and undo.
- **D265** (SDLC stage): DESIGN
- **E265** (Kind): DESIGN
- **F265** (Test type): _empty_
- **G265** (Plugin): unbreakable
- **H265** (Location): pure-prose/unbreakable/be-reasonable/SKILL.md § 1.2 Lean toward the cheaper mistake
- **I265** (Trigger): uncertain choice

## Group 49 (starts row 274)

- **F274** (Test type): _empty_
- **G274** (Plugin): unbreakable

- **I274** (Trigger): decision pulled by two concerns

- **A275** (Group plugin): unbreakable
- **B275** (New rule): _empty_

- **A308** (Group plugin): machinery
- **B308** (New rule): When dispatching, use the cheapest model that can do that kind of work and hold quality through checks on the result: cheapest tier to enumerate or extract with citations, mid tier for synthesis and judgement, a fixed top tier above sonnet for design, adjudication, verdicts and coding (never "whatever the main conversation runs"). A plan names the model for each kind of task. [OPEN: the invariant auditor is given "the judgement tier" (M444) but issues verdicts (M5 top tier) — owner decides]

## Group 70 (starts row 312)

### Row 312

- **A312** (Group plugin): machinery
- **B312** (New rule): Per owner ruling, create on demand a pool of one agent per kind of task (standing kinds: doing the work, reviewing it, filing rules; only the owner names a new kind), reuse that agent serially for every dispatch of its kind, including dependent tasks, and reap and replace it when its context grows to a concerning size. Kinds may run concurrently; within a kind, dispatch, wait, check the result, then dispatch the next; independence is not permission to parallelise, and batching changes dispatch size, never concurrency. Before dispatching, check the live-agents view; name each agent at spawn for its kind and subject; every dispatch forbids spawning sub-agents; anything that fans out by construction runs only when the user explicitly asks. [Superseded by owner ruling: agent pool reused serially and autoreaped, replacing "a fresh agent per task is the exception" (M18)]
- **C312** (Existing rule): M18: - Dependent tasks go to one agent in sequence; a fresh agent per task is the exception, and the dispatch that uses it says so.
- **D312** (SDLC stage): PLAN
- **E312** (Kind): AGENTS
- **F312** (Test type): _empty_
- **G312** (Plugin): machinery
- **H312** (Location): plugins/machinery/rules/agent-topology.md § Batching
- **I312** (Trigger): dependent tasks

## Group 71 (starts row 321)

### Row 321

- **A321** (Group plugin): machinery
- **B321** (New rule): When a long job is made of many self-contained pieces, dispatch it in batches (not one agent per piece, not all in one); pieces share a batch only if they have no ordering or shared state between them. Report how many batches ran and what each covered.
- **C321** (Existing rule): M14: - A long job made of many self-contained pieces is dispatched in batches. One agent per piece pays the setup cost over and over; all of it in one agent buries the late pieces and loses everything on a single failure.

## Group 72 (starts row 324)

### Row 324

- **A324** (Group plugin): machinery
- **B324** (New rule): When a scan, extraction or search harvests candidates for agents or action, pass them through a triage gate that keeps only those whose answer would change a decision (never a fixed count or fraction), and report what was dropped, by category, on every run. A scan hit or audit finding is a suspicion: act on it only after a person has read the code and recorded a confirmation naming the exact place.
- **C324** (Existing rule): M37: - Fan-out removes the cost constraint on spending agents; it does not remove the obligation to aim them. A fleet of agents sent after something that cannot change what happens next is not thoroughness — it is attention spent where there was nothing to find.
- **D324** (SDLC stage): PLAN
- **E324** (Kind): AGENTS
- **F324** (Test type): _empty_
- **G324** (Plugin): machinery
- **H324** (Location): plugins/machinery/rules/agent-topology.md § Aiming a fan-out
- **I324** (Trigger): planning a fan-out

### Row 325 (new rule in B324)

- **A325** (Group plugin): machinery
- **B325** (New rule): _empty_
- **C325** (Existing rule): M38: - A triage gate sits between whatever harvests candidates — a scan, an extraction pass, a search — and whatever fans agents out to work them. Nothing reaches an agent without passing through that gate first, and what it keeps is only the candidates whose outcome would actually change a decision.
- **D325** (SDLC stage): PLAN
- **E325** (Kind): AGENTS
- **F325** (Test type): MANUAL
- **G325** (Plugin): machinery

- **I325** (Trigger): a harvest feeds a fan-out

- **B326** (New rule): _empty_

- **D326** (SDLC stage): PLAN
- **E326** (Kind): AGENTS
- **F326** (Test type): MANUAL

## Group 76 (starts row 339)

### Row 339

- **A339** (Group plugin): machinery
- **B339** (New rule): Before writing code, working across a design, or acting outside your own working copy, read the current authority fresh (the infrastructure the code runs against, the owning design, the environment's real target, a harness's own configuration) instead of recalling it or trusting a copied document; only specifications loaded with the project are exempt. An unratified record describes current behaviour but leaves its own open questions open. An undecided design question is filed as work and the agent stops: never blocked on, silently resolved or improvised.
- **C339** (Existing rule): M250: - Before you write code, make sure your design intent matches the existing infrastructure it will run against — read that infrastructure rather than recalling it. No implementation detail is delivered from memory alone; the sole exception is a specification loaded with the project. (Gabe, 2026-09-06, URULE.)
- **D339** (SDLC stage): PRE-CODE
- **E339** (Kind): PLANNING
- **F339** (Test type): MANUAL
- **G339** (Plugin): machinery

- **I339** (Trigger): before writing code

## Group 77 (starts row 345)

### Row 345

- **A345** (Group plugin): machinery
- **B345** (New rule): When optimising, locating a boundary, or comparing performance: measure instead of reasoning, including the tail, not only the mean; before reusing a work-skipping technique, measure what fraction of inputs at the new site can be skipped. To compare builds, build both first, then alternate runs and report every round, on a quiet machine, stating the configuration; isolate variables one at a time before trusting an effect.
- **C345** (Existing rule): M248: - Before reusing a technique that works by skipping work, measure what fraction of the inputs at the new site can actually be skipped. That fraction decides whether it helps, not the fact that the code looks the same shape.

- **E345** (Kind): TESTING

## Group 80 (starts row 361)

### Row 361

- **A361** (Group plugin): machinery
- **B361** (New rule): At most one agent works in a working copy unless the owner asks otherwise. Anything that reviews, exercises or measures in-progress work (a reviewing agent, a stand-in service, a performance run) runs from its own copy of that branch, never the shared copy or the copy being edited; removing a copy does not stop a process started from it. Temporary measuring code placed where others can see it is announced first (file and a unique marker) and both are removed when the measurement ends. [Resolves conflict: M241, M296 — own copy is the default; announce only when unavoidable; owner to confirm]
- **C361** (Existing rule): M22: - A stand-in service started for a piece of work runs from that work's own working copy, never the shared one and never shared between jobs, so it serves the code under test. This gives the process a visible owner but does not stop it: removing the working copy does not kill what was started from it.
- **D361** (SDLC stage): IMPL
- **E361** (Kind): WORKTREE
- **F361** (Test type): E2E
- **G361** (Plugin): machinery
- **H361** (Location): plugins/machinery/rules/agent-topology.md § Where an agent works
- **I361** (Trigger): starting a stand-in service

### Row 362 (new rule in B361)

- **A362** (Group plugin): machinery
- **B362** (New rule): _empty_
- **C362** (Existing rule): M296: - A working copy someone else can see is shared state. Announce temporary measuring code before you write it — the file, and a unique marker to find it by — and remove both when the measurement ends. Unannounced, live instrumentation is indistinguishable from unexplained drift, and the honest response to unexplained drift is to revert it, which destroys the measurement mid-run.

- **E362** (Kind): INSTRUMENTATION
- **F362** (Test type): PERF

## Group 81 (starts row 367)

### Row 367

- **A367** (Group plugin): machinery
- **B367** (New rule): When writing an assertion, test at the level where the bug would live, against the contract. Take the measurement from the system under test and the expectation from something it never produced (the stated setting, fixture dimensions, input shape, arithmetic). An output-capturing test drives the real renderer by the same path. A number copied from a run is a labelled regression pin, never coverage; where no independent expectation exists yet, say so at the assertion and leave the work open. A suite that cannot express a failure is not coverage for it. [Resolves conflict: M227 — one renderer, so no equivalence test between two; owner to confirm]
- **C367** (Existing rule): M66: - The rule against a second opinion governs the working path and inverts inside a test's expected value: in the system a second opinion is a defect, but on the other side of an assertion it is the whole point. An expectation taken from the thing under test can only report that it changed, never that it is wrong.
- **D367** (SDLC stage): IMPL
- **E367** (Kind): TESTING
- **F367** (Test type): UNIT
- **G367** (Plugin): machinery
- **H367** (Location): plugins/machinery/rules/design-invariants.md § Measurement and expectation
- **I367** (Trigger): writing a test expectation

### Row 368 (new rule in B367)

- **A368** (Group plugin): machinery
- **B368** (New rule): _empty_
- **C368** (Existing rule): M67: - Take the measurement from the system under test, and the expectation from something the system never produced: the stated setting, the fixture's own dimensions, the input's own shape, or arithmetic. With both sides from the system you have only proved it agrees with itself, and an error affecting everything passes.
- **D368** (SDLC stage): IMPL
- **E368** (Kind): TESTING
- **F368** (Test type): UNIT
- **G368** (Plugin): machinery
- **H368** (Location): plugins/machinery/rules/design-invariants.md § Measurement and expectation
- **I368** (Trigger): writing a test assertion

## Group 82 (starts row 374)

### Row 374

- **A374** (Group plugin): unbreakable
- **B374** (New rule): When enforcing an invariant, use the strongest tools of the language at hand, per the skill's language table (e.g. Rust enums, newtypes and private fields; TypeScript branded types and never-exhaustiveness; Python frozen dataclasses, assert_never and strict mypy in CI; SQL constraints and writes via views). Shell, YAML and config have none: generate them from a typed source and treat hand-edited config as untrusted input; FFI and wire formats get generated typed wrappers behind one marshalling choke point.
- **C374** (Existing rule): U33: | **Rust** | enums + exhaustive `match`, ownership, newtypes, private fields + smart constructors, sealed traits, typestate (`PhantomData`), `#[must_use]`, `#[non_exhaustive]`, `Send`/`Sync` |

## Group 88 (starts row 402)

### Row 402

- **A402** (Group plugin): machinery
- **B402** (New rule): New code ships with instrumentation, and a change that touches an uninstrumented site adds it (no retrofit sweeps): non-trivial functions added or touched get the profiling annotation; each new decision site gets a diagnostic switch or trace row, inert until turned on and registered in the diagnostics index in the same change, recording the decision and the values actually used (never recomputed), its denominator and explicit zeros, shaped to the question, with a positive control. A unit declaring itself profiled instruments something or sits on a shrink-only gap list. A change is done only when its sites can be interrogated without editing code.
- **C402** (Existing rule): M64: - A diagnostic dump shows the values the system actually used, never a recomputed equivalent. Otherwise the work is judged against something the system never saw.

## Group 91 (starts row 418)

### Row 418

- **A418** (Group plugin): machinery
- **B418** (New rule): The commit gate enforces filing (register-check, spec-check): a commit is refused while any rule or spec inbox entry is undispositioned or a spec disposition points outside docs/dictated-specs; it runs on every commit and again in CI, where the hosted check blocks and the branch is protected on it. Never carry an undispositioned entry across a commit even where no gate runs. Bypass with --no-verify only in a genuine emergency; needing it twice means fix the checker. Editing a rule file without the register prints an advisory nudge. Before calling a filing done, run the complete register check, not the commit subset.
- **C418** (Existing rule): M167: - Editing a rule-bearing document while the register sits untouched prints a reminder to update the register in the same commit. It is advisory and never blocks.
- **D418** (SDLC stage): IMPL
- **E418** (Kind): RULE-PROCESS
- **F418** (Test type): SOURCE-SCAN
- **G418** (Plugin): machinery
- **H418** (Location): plugins/machinery/rules/rule-governance.md § Honesty about the machinery
- **I418** (Trigger): rule document edited, register untouched

## Group 93 (starts row 433)

### Row 433

- **A433** (Group plugin): unbreakable
- **B433** (New rule): When work produces a tool with large or repeated output (script, diagnostic, migration, report generator), the assistant builds it and writes one usage document as it is built (invocation, modes, normal output, what to bring back when wrong), and the developer runs it and reports back; checks you can do in one cheap call you do yourself. Large results live in a file with only the verdict in the conversation; read source by searching first, then the range that matters. Spend context on small dense things that prevent errors, not large low-density ones.
- **C433** (Existing rule): U169: | Usage instructions | one consolidated document, written as the tool is built | a conversation is compacted and lost at the session boundary and then re-derived; a document survives and is read selectively |

## Group 94 (starts row 441)

### Row 441

- **A441** (Group plugin): machinery
- **B441** (New rule): When running a suite, gate or harness, run all of it without stopping at the first failure and keep its complete, untruncated output; never pass a tool its quiet flag under the output filter. A tool whose summary does not match the proof format prints an extra conforming count line, and a gate prints each sub-check's count line on its own output. [OPEN: whether the full output is read in full into context (M425) or kept in a file with only the proof lines read (U168) — owner decides]
- **C441** (Existing rule): M183: - A gate that runs sub-checks and sends their output to a log still prints each sub-check's own count line on its own output. Pass or fail alone is not enough: a failure stays visible anyway, and a pass for a bad reason is exactly the thing that gets compressed away.

- **E441** (Kind): TOOLING
- **F441** (Test type): GATE

## Group 95 (starts row 446)

- **B446** (New rule): When writing a record, comment or generated code, use the fewest lines a cold reader can act on, saying what the artifact cannot: why, what else was considered, what is still wrong; never restate the artifact or the diff. ## Group 98 (starts row 467)

### Row 467

- **A467** (Group plugin): tie: machinery / developer-friendliness
- **B467** (New rule): After implementing, before calling it done, confirm each written prediction one by one and say whether it held; an unconfirmed one means not done, and a failed one is reported at full strength naming whether the work or the belief was wrong. Keep the effort's outcome ledger as you go, and when the result is measurable record whether it achieved its point: what changed, improved, regressed or is newly wrong, new smells, accepted regressions with why, and plain failure to meet the goal. [Resolves conflict: M330, D62 — capture as you go; outcome judged when measurable; owner to confirm]
- **C467** (Existing rule): M330: 6. **Keep the effort's ledger as you go** — what you did, what changed, what got better, what regressed, whether it worked, and what smells new. That list is the `developer-friendliness` skill's § 6.4 Outcomes, which this plugin depends on and which loads in every session; machinery's own `rules/work-tracking.md` § The learnings record carries the rest — the ledger ships with the effort, not after it, and every entry stands on its own.

## Group 99 (starts row 475)

### Row 475

- **A475** (Group plugin): machinery
- **B475** (New rule): For every fix, new code path, and new check: write the test first, run it and watch it fail for the expected reason, then write the smallest code that makes it pass; that red run before the code exists is the proof the test can fail. A check whose target is one real file gets its red run against a fixture copy before the check exists. A site you believe is affected that the build did not name gets its path written down and a failing test there before you touch it. [Superseded by owner ruling: TDD order — no implement-then-break, and no disturbing and restoring real files (M279)]
- **C475** (Existing rule): M209: - A site you believe is affected that neither the compiler nor a test named gets the path written down first, from the change to that site, and a test that fails there before you touch it.
- **D475** (SDLC stage): IMPL
- **E475** (Kind): TESTING
- **F475** (Test type): REGRESSION
- **G475** (Plugin): machinery
- **H475** (Location): plugins/machinery/rules/verification-and-evidence.md § The word you just wrote makes a check due
- **I475** (Trigger): believed-affected site not named by build

## Group 101 (starts row 485)

### Row 485

- **A485** (Group plugin): machinery
- **B485** (New rule): A search-based check ships a case proving it still matches; a test asserting something did not happen says what shows its observer could have seen it; a test guarding how an algorithm scales asserts the work done against what its input entitles it to, with a positive control, never a time limit (a generous timeout may remain only as a hang backstop).
- **C485** (Existing rule): M76: - A check built on searching always ships a case proving it still matches. One that has quietly stopped matching looks exactly like a codebase that complies.
- **D485** (SDLC stage): IMPL
- **E485** (Kind): TESTING
- **F485** (Test type): META
- **G485** (Plugin): machinery
- **H485** (Location): plugins/machinery/rules/design-invariants.md § One authority per switch
- **I485** (Trigger): search-based check

### Row 486 (new rule in B485)

- **A486** (Group plugin): machinery
- **B486** (New rule): _empty_
- **C486** (Existing rule): M226: - Asserting that something did not happen only counts if the observer was capable of seeing it happen. Say what would have proved the observer was alive.
- **D486** (SDLC stage): IMPL
- **E486** (Kind): TESTING
- **F486** (Test type): META
- **G486** (Plugin): machinery
- **H486** (Location): plugins/machinery/rules/verification-and-evidence.md § What a test can honestly claim
- **I486** (Trigger): asserting something did not happen

### Row 487 (new rule in B485)

- **A487** (Group plugin): machinery
- **B487** (New rule): _empty_
- **C487** (Existing rule): M229: - A test that guards how an algorithm scales asserts the work it actually did, measured against what its input entitles it to, never a time limit. On a shared machine a stopwatch measures the machine, and cannot tell a busy one from an algorithm that got slower. A generous time limit may stay as a backstop against hanging, never as the verdict, and the work count ships with a positive control, because something that never ran satisfies any upper bound for free.
- **D487** (SDLC stage): IMPL
- **E487** (Kind): TESTING
- **F487** (Test type): PERF / META
- **G487** (Plugin): machinery
- **H487** (Location): plugins/machinery/rules/verification-and-evidence.md § What a test can honestly claim
- **I487** (Trigger): test guards algorithm scaling

## Group 112 (starts row 503)

### Row 503

- **A503** (Group plugin): tie: machinery / developer-friendliness
- **B503** (New rule): Per owner ruling, reports are a few lines, exhaustive only within the task's blast radius. Lead with the answer and any bad news: a failure, regression, or thing left not building or running comes first, at full strength, in the first message after you know. Before doing anything that differs from what was agreed, stop and say so. Disclose rather than narrate: name scope cut, steps skipped (with why), what could not be done (with what was tried), work beyond the ask, and anything skipped or unverified (unproven, never passed). A per-task report in a multi-task effort is its test-result lines and deviations only. Honesty outranks looking competent.
- **C503** (Existing rule): M276: and its per-task report is the test-result lines and deviations only.
- **D503** (SDLC stage): POST-IMPL
- **E503** (Kind): REPORTING
- **F503** (Test type): CLAIM-CHECK

## Group 114 (starts row 520)

### Row 520

- **A520** (Group plugin): machinery
- **B520** (New rule): Before merging anything that could move output (engine, algorithm or performance rewrites, changed defaults) or that claims output is unchanged, run the comparison agent: build what the harness compares, produce the output and diff its failure set against an established baseline, never trusting the exit code or the unit suite alone; if a difference cannot be attributed, label the run unbaselined rather than guess. Classify every non-informational row as newly broken, already broken or newly fixed. A person reviews every regenerated baseline line by line, and a correctness question found in a performance change gets its own change, data and comparison.
- **C520** (Existing rule): M200: - A claim that output is unchanged is measured by producing the output and comparing it. It is never assumed.
- **D520** (SDLC stage): POST-IMPL
- **E520** (Kind): TESTING
- **F520** (Test type): BASELINE
- **G520** (Plugin): machinery
- **H520** (Location): plugins/machinery/rules/verification-and-evidence.md § Declare the standard you are claiming
- **I520** (Trigger): claiming output is unchanged

- **A521** (Group plugin): machinery
- **B521** (New rule): _empty_

- **B533** (New rule): RULES_INDEX.md and SPEC_INDEX.md (universal under register/, project under .claude/machinery/) are generated and never hand-edited. ## Group 117 (starts row 547)

- **B547** (New rule): Never file or adjudicate a rule, or decide a supersession of the owner's rule, yourself: every candidate rule, and every instruction that proved wrong, goes to the owner as a proposal through the project's process, never silently worked around. ## Group 121 (starts row 568)

### Row 568

- **A568** (Group plugin): machinery
- **B568** (New rule): When a wrapped command's output ends with a [quiet:train] line, or to batch-train a tool from stored logs, run /machinery:train-tool: read the named log, pick the one line stating the tool's answer (not an error, the last line, or progress), and run train-tool.mjs identify with that line number (batch: list logs with the logs command, identify oldest first). The matcher is derived from your picks, never hand-written. Commit a graduated catalog entry with its frozen fixture. If graduation is refused for a fixture reason keep identifying on later runs; for any other reason, or if the answer line changes shape between runs, stop and tell the owner. If the log listing is empty, check it looked in the directory the runner writes to.
- **C568** (Existing rule): M513: commit both: the learned matcher and its frozen fixture are a team artifact, like the rest of the project catalog
- **D568** (SDLC stage): COMMIT
- **E568** (Kind): TOOLING
- **F568** (Test type): _empty_

## Group 124 (starts row 585)

### Row 585

- **A585** (Group plugin): machinery
- **B585** (New rule): When a dispatch falls under an owner ruling, pass the ruling verbatim (its words or its document), never a paraphrase, and never license deviation or call it a sketch or proposal; state which outcomes are unavailable and which arguments were already rejected. An agent that reports departing from a ruling goes back to the owner; the dispatcher never accepts the departure on the owner's behalf.
- **C585** (Existing rule): M48: - An agent reporting that it departed from a ruling goes back to the owner; whoever dispatched it never accepts the departure as engineering judgement on the owner's behalf. Each acceptance becomes precedent inside the effort, so the next departure arrives looking better justified than the last, and the ruling is gone before anyone notices it was contradicted.
- **D585** (SDLC stage): REVIEW
- **E585** (Kind): AGENTS
- **F585** (Test type): _empty_
- **G585** (Plugin): machinery

- **A586** (Group plugin): machinery
- **B586** (New rule): _empty_
- **C586** (Existing rule): M46: - A decision the owner has already made reaches a dispatched agent verbatim — the ruling's own words, or the document carrying them — never your paraphrase of it. A restatement carries none of the ruling's authority, so an agent that meets friction weighs your summary against the code in front of it, and the code wins.

Telling an agent that departures are fine, or calling the decision a sketch, a plan or a proposal, converts it into one. State instead which outcomes are not available, and name the arguments already considered and rejected, so the agent cannot rediscover them and present them as new.

## Group 130 (starts row 624)

### Row 624

- **A624** (Group plugin): machinery
- **B624** (New rule): The invariant auditor reads only the ledger parts covering the change, then for each changed region finds claims by their vocabulary (by construction, invariant, must never, guaranteed, sole, caller must, precondition...) and rates the mechanism, not the sentence, never inventing unstated claims. It runs the second-call-site tripwire and flags catalogue shapes with a live example each. Its denominator is generated from the material: every stated claim (is there a mechanism?) and every obligation shape (stated? enforced?) from the standing list (sibling-field docs, a fact stored twice, kind plus kind-specific fields, magic or clamped values, unchecked first/last, raw numbers where a type exists, adjacent same-typed parameters, unenforced call order, discarded results), including dead or test-only code outside shipped units.
- **C624** (Existing rule): M446: 1. **Read the enforcement ledger first**, loading only the parts covering what changed. The ledger holds *claimed* invariants — properties the source asserts to a reader. A property that is true but was never stated is not a row, and the auditor must not invent one.
- **D624** (SDLC stage): REVIEW
- **E624** (Kind): AGENTS
- **F624** (Test type): _empty_
- **G624** (Plugin): machinery
- **H624** (Location): plugins/machinery/agents/invariant-auditor.md § Procedure
- **I624** (Trigger): starting an audit

The claim is whatever the source promises a reader.

- **D631** (SDLC stage): REVIEW
- **E631** (Kind): AGENTS

### Row 632 (new rule in B624)

- **A632** (Group plugin): machinery
- **B632** (New rule): _empty_
- **C632** (Existing rule): M464: - The standing obligation shapes are: a field whose documentation names a sibling field; the same fact stored twice, such as a count kept beside the collection it counts or a value kept beside a variant of itself; a record holding a kind alongside fields meaningful for only some of those kinds; a comparison against a magic value or a domain value clamped at zero; taking the first or last element of a collection with a call that fails when it is empty, where nothing proves it is not; a quantity carried as a raw number where a dedicated type already exists; a public function with two or more adjacent parameters of the same simple type, which a caller can transpose without complaint; public methods that begin, initialise, set, finalise, end or reset something mutable, a required order that nothing enforces; and a call whose result is thrown away or quietly replaced by a default, so a failure has nowhere to be seen.
- **D632** (SDLC stage): REVIEW
- **E632** (Kind): AGENTS
- **F632** (Test type): _empty_
- **G632** (Plugin): machinery
- **H632** (Location): plugins/machinery/agents/invariant-auditor.md § Auditing invariants: the denominator
- **I632** (Trigger): scanning for obligation shapes

## Group 132 (starts row 642)

### Row 642

- **A642** (Group plugin): machinery
- **B642** (New rule): The audit's denominator generator is deterministic (sorted) and sanitises its evidence text; the output table is checked structurally (column count, unique ids, ids in the denominator, printed row count and remainder) and every actionable finding maps to exactly one fix. It produces a ledger table dispositioning every entry and a deduplicated fix list whose order is labelled as the one judgement call; enforced or retired findings are counts, undetectable shapes are declared blocked, and the report states what the method cannot see and that row counts are not effort.

## Group 133 (starts row 651)

### Row 651

- **A651** (Group plugin): machinery
- **B651** (New rule): When ending an effort, merge locally in the project root into a private result, run the merge gate and every required verification leg on it, and push only when all are green; discard a red result. Green is necessary but not sufficient: nothing a user can reach may be broken or unfinished, and incomplete code merges only when nothing in production can call it; an effort may land in pieces that each clear this bar. Exploration branches are merged only by deliberate decision. The effort-lifecycle skill holds the steps.
- **C651** (Existing rule): M307: - The merge is made locally first, into a private merge result nobody else can see, because the gate judges that result and it has to exist to be judged. Publish only when every required verification leg on it is green; a red result is discarded, never pushed. Passing one leg is not passing, and a merge that exists only locally is not yet a merge anyone has to live with — which is exactly what makes discarding it cheap.
- **D651** (SDLC stage): MERGE
- **E651** (Kind): WORKTREE
- **F651** (Test type): GATE
- **G651** (Plugin): machinery
- **H651** (Location): plugins/machinery/rules/worktree-discipline.md § Merging and tearing down
- **I651** (Trigger): merging an effort

### Row 652 (new rule in B651)

- **A652** (Group plugin): machinery
- **B652** (New rule): _empty_
- **C652** (Existing rule): M308: - Branches kept for exploration are never merged automatically. Merging one is always a deliberate decision.
- **D652** (SDLC stage): MERGE
- **E652** (Kind): WORKTREE
- **F652** (Test type): _empty_
- **G652** (Plugin): machinery
- **H652** (Location): plugins/machinery/rules/worktree-discipline.md § Merging and tearing down
- **I652** (Trigger): exploration branch

- **A653** (Group plugin): machinery
- **B653** (New rule): _empty_
- **C653** (Existing rule): M313: - Only code that is not broken and will not cause problems is merged into the shared line. A green gate is necessary and not sufficient: the gate measures tests and invariants, and this bar is about what a user can reach and what happens when they do. (Gabe, 2026-09-05, URULE.)
- **D653** (SDLC stage): MERGE
- **E653** (Kind): WORKTREE
- **F653** (Test type): GATE / E2E

- **B654** (New rule): _empty_

Incomplete code a user CAN reach — a control that does nothing, a default configuration that warns on every layer, a path that answers wrongly — is not mergeable until it is finished or made unreachable.

Run the `merge-gate` skill (spec 2 — until it exists, run the project's own verification legs on the merged result); the bar it enforces is `rules/worktree-discipline.md` § Merging and tearing down. ## Group 134 (starts row 658)

### Row 658

- **A658** (Group plugin): machinery
- **B658** (New rule): When merging the primary line into a long-lived edition branch throws a wall of conflicts, or both have rewritten the same shared parts, use the refresh-diverged-branch procedure (read its premise first) instead of ordinary syncing, which stays the default until it costs more. ## Group 135 (starts row 664)

### Row 664

- **A664** (Group plugin): machinery
- **B664** (New rule): When refreshing a diverged branch, follow the skill's steps: diff each side against the common ancestor (never tip to tip); bin files as edition-only or both-changed; triage both-changed by direction (identical: nothing; primary far ahead: keep primary and graft the small hook; comparable: real divergence; docs take both; lock files regenerate). Start from a fresh copy of the primary line, re-apply edition-only files verbatim, graft only the blocks carrying the edition's intent, and re-home edition-only files into the edition's own module behind a shared interface, never a conditional-compilation switch.
- **C664** (Existing rule): M354: 1. **Find the common ancestor of the two branches, and list what each side changed against it — never one tip against the other.** A tip-to-tip comparison reports a file as newly added when both sides added it independently, which hides a real collision as a one-sided change. You end this step holding two lists: the files the primary line changed since the ancestor, and the files the edition branch changed since the ancestor.
- **D664** (SDLC stage): MERGE
- **E664** (Kind): WORKTREE
- **F664** (Test type): _empty_
- **G664** (Plugin): machinery
- **H664** (Location): claude-code/machinery/refresh-diverged-branch/SKILL.md § The steps
- **I664** (Trigger): refreshing a diverged branch

- **B665** (New rule): _empty_
- **C665** (Existing rule): M355: 2. **From the two lists made in step 1, form two bins.** The *one-sided* bin is every file only the edition branch changed. The *both-changed* bin is every file that appears on both lists.
- **D665** (SDLC stage): MERGE
- **E665** (Kind): WORKTREE
- **F665** (Test type): _empty_
- **G665** (Plugin): machinery
- **H665** (Location): claude-code/machinery/refresh-diverged-branch/SKILL.md § The steps
- **I665** (Trigger): refreshing a diverged branch

Each goes into that edition's own module, never back into shared code behind a conditional-compilation switch. The shared side keeps the interface; the edition supplies the implementation, and consumers' imports are updated to match. This is what stops that file from being a shared-file conflict at every future refresh, so it is not optional tidying.

## Group 145 (starts row 700)

### Row 700

- **A700** (Group plugin): machinery
- **B700** (New rule): When you write "class", "confirmed", "closed", or that tooling exists, blocks or would catch something, the matching check is due before you send it. For a class: search the area you changed and fix or defer each instance by name; instances outside the task's blast radius are filed with the campaign label and the follow-up label, not chased. For tooling: run the check that could prove you wrong and say where it does and does not run. For "closed", or a fix you doubt: write what would have to be true, in a form a later reader can check and find false, and file a doubted fix with it.
- **C700** (Existing rule): M168: - State exactly where the gate does and does not run, in the same breath as claiming it exists. An opt-in hook and a missing continuous check are both holes, and naming them is part of the claim.

- **B764** (New rule): _empty_

- **D764** (SDLC stage): ANY
- **E764** (Kind): COMMUNICATION
- **F764** (Test type): _empty_
- **G764** (Plugin): machinery
- **H764** (Location): plugins/machinery/rules/straight-talk.md § How much to say

### Row 765 (new rule in B763)

- **A765** (Group plugin): machinery
- **B765** (New rule): _empty_
- **C765** (Existing rule): M182: - Speak in standard coding and technology vocabulary — nouns, verbs and adjectives alike. This is a method of that class; A is a subclass of B; this member is public when it should be private; the field is encapsulated; the constructor is package-private; the member is protected. Never introduce an invented idiom or metaphor for a code structure, or for an operation on one, unless the user has agreed to that term first. Shared vocabulary a developer already has is not the jargon the plain-analogy rule targets — that rule is for genuinely esoteric domain terms, and it is not a licence to coin metaphors for ordinary code structure. (Gabe, 2026-09-08, URULE.)
- **D765** (SDLC stage): ANY
- **E765** (Kind): COMMUNICATION
- **F765** (Test type): _empty_
- **G765** (Plugin): machinery
- **H765** (Location): plugins/machinery/rules/straight-talk.md § The words you use
- **I765** (Trigger): describing code structure

## Group 158 (starts row 766)

### Row 766

- **A766** (Group plugin): developer-friendliness
- **B766** (New rule): Throughout a session, whenever you learn, decide, defer, break, cut, verify or finish something, ask at that moment: will someone need it again (it cost effort and is not visible from the artifact: a surprise, something you had to run to learn, a project fact not derivable), who reads it and when (no nameable reader, no record; "for completeness" is not one), and is writing cheaper than reconstructing. If not needed, say it once and let it go; scaffolding gets nothing. Otherwise put it where the project already keeps that kind of thing, never a second place, at the least durable rung that survives your absence rather than what every session loads. Test: if the session ended now, what would have to be reconstructed, and by whom?
- **C766** (Existing rule): U173: | Durable decisions and conventions | written down, in the least durable place that still survives the reader's absence | conversation is not memory and documents are — but a footnote parked in what every session loads is a tax every session pays |
- **D766** (SDLC stage): ANY
- **E766** (Kind): RECORDS
- **F766** (Test type): _empty_
- **G766** (Plugin): unbreakable
- **H766** (Location): pure-prose/unbreakable/be-reasonable/SKILL.md § 6.7 Working under a context budget | Durable decisions and conventions
- **I766** (Trigger): durable decision or convention

### Row 767 (new rule in B766)

- **A767** (Group plugin): developer-friendliness
- **B767** (New rule): _empty_
- **C767** (Existing rule): D1: Use throughout any session working with a developer — governs everything you produce that outlives the conversation: what you file, write down, report, read back, and leave behind at a session boundary. Not the ergonomics of the code you ship; the ergonomics of working with you. The developer should never have to hold what you could have parked, and should never pay full price to get it back — under a budget that makes over-recording (a tracker nobody triages, a notes file nobody opens) as much of a failure as writing nothing. Includes a fully worked session showing what gets recorded and what deliberately does not, the three-question filter, the durability ladder whose top rung is deleting the note by making the situation impossible, the retrieval ladder for reading a record without paying for all of it, domain tables for deferred work, learnings, decisions, outcomes, continuity, surprise and record drift, red-flag phrases, what to do when the record you inherit is already broken, and two tripwires: you just re-derived something you knew in an earlier session, and you are writing a record whose reader you cannot name. Use whenever you find, defer, decide, break, learn, verify, or finish something — not only when asked to write documentation
- **D767** (SDLC stage): ANY
- **E767** (Kind): SKILL-TRIGGER
- **F767** (Test type): _empty_
- **G767** (Plugin): developer-friendliness
- **H767** (Location): claude-code/developer-friendliness/developer-friendliness/SKILL.md § frontmatter
- **I767** (Trigger): any session with a developer; find/defer/decide/break/learn/verify/finish

## Group 160 (starts row 794)

### Row 794

- **A794** (Group plugin): developer-friendliness
- **B794** (New rule): Capture a detail when it happens (a line in your task report is enough); only the full write-up may wait, for one pass before the work lands. "I'll mention it at the end", "I'll remember" and "as I said earlier" mean it is already being lost.
- **C794** (Existing rule): D30: - **Capture at the moment; write up in one pass.** Capturing a detail and writing it up are two acts, and only the first is urgent. Capture when it happens — a line in your own report of the task is enough — because the detail that mattered, the exact error, the thing you tried first, is the part you have already forgotten by the end. The full write-up can wait for one pass before the work lands. Batching the *capture* is what costs: it pays to reconstruct everything batched, out of the next reader's hour rather than your minute.
- **D794** (SDLC stage): ANY
- **E794** (Kind): RECORDS
- **F794** (Test type): _empty_
- **G794** (Plugin): developer-friendliness
- **H794** (Location): claude-code/developer-friendliness/developer-friendliness/SKILL.md § 5.1 Writing
- **I794** (Trigger): detail worth keeping occurs
